A unified approach to coupled homogeneous linear wave propagation in generic gravity
Lucas T. Santana, João C. Lobato, Ribamar R. R. Reis, Maurício O. Calvão

Abstract
Wave propagation is a common occurrence in all of physics. A linear approximation provides a simpler way to describe various fields related to observable phenomena in laboratory physics as well as astronomy and cosmology, allowing us to probe gravitation through its effect on the trajectories of particles associated with those fields. This paper proposes a unified framework to describe the wave propagation of a set of interacting tensor fields that obey coupled homogeneous linear second-order partial differential equations for arbitrary curved spacetimes, both Lorentzian and metric-affine. We use JWKB Ansätze for all fields, written in terms of a perturbation parameter proportional to a representative wavelength among them, deriving a set of hierarchical algebraic and differential equations that link the fields’ phases and different order amplitudes. This allows us to reobtain the well-known laws of geometrical optics and beyond geometrical optics in a generalized form, showing that these laws are independent of the rank of the fields involved. This is true as long as what we refer to as the kinetic tensor of a given field satisfies a set of diagonality conditions, which further imply a handful of simplifications on the transport equations obtained in the subleading orders of the JWKB Ansätze. We explore these results in several notable examples in Lorentzian and metric-affine spacetimes, illustrating the reach of our derivations in general relativity, reduced Horndeski theories, spacetimes with completely antisymmetric torsion and Weyl spacetimes. The formalism presented herein lays the groundwork for the study of rays associated with different types of waves in curved spacetimes and provides the tools to compute modifications to their brightness evolution laws, consequential distance duality relations, and beyond geometrical optics phenomena.



I
Introduction

Describing the propagation of linear waves in curved spacetimes is of uttermost importance to model several physical phenomena of relevance to modern cosmology and astrophysics, such as gravitational lensing
[
1
]
and the propagation of gravitational waves
[
2
]
. But describing (scalar, vector, tensor, etc.) linear waves associated to a physical field directly by means of the corresponding equations of motion in curved spacetimes is generally a difficult endeavor, and different workarounds are usually employed to tackle this issue in a simpler or more tractable form. For one, there is the study of discontinuities in a field by means of so-called
characteristic surfaces
and
bi-characteristic curves
in general
[
3
,
4
,
5
,
6
,
7
,
8
,
9
]
as well as in the case,
e.g.
, of modified gravity
[
10
,
11
]
. Additionally,
Fourier transforms
allow us to study the oscillation modes of a physical field (
e.g.
the electromagnetic field)
[
8
,
12
]
, although such a decomposition might only be possible in specific spacetimes possessing a number of symmetries.

Of particular relevance is an alternative procedure given by an approximation scheme where a field is associated to a phase function and a family of amplitudes, combined in a (formal) Jeffreys-Wentzel-Kramers-Brillouin (JWKB) expansion [cf. Eqs. (
14
)]
[
13
,
14
,
15
,
1
,
16
,
17
,
18
]
. This relies on well-known observables reminiscent from general physics, such as wavelength, intensity, and polarization. In particular, the wavelength of a wave—inherently associated to a reference frame—introduces a characteristic scale allowing for two crude regimes of wave propagation to be identified: (i) one in which the wavelength is much smaller than any other length scale in the region where the wave is passing by, and (ii) another where the wavelength is of the order of (or bigger than) some (or all) length scales in the considered region. The former assumes that the wave character of the field might essentialy be disregarded, and a family of rays is identified as the trajectories traversed by corresponding fiducial particles. These rays are geometrical paths in spacetime, which make this regime generally referred to as
geometrical optics
(GO) or
eikonal
or
high frequency approximation
[cf. Eqs. (
16a
) and (
16b
)], a nomenclature common to electromagnetic waves and also used when referring to scalar fields and gravitational waves
[
19
,
20
]
. In contrast, the second regime, in which the wave character of a field should be more thoroughly taken into account, is usually referred to as
beyond geometrical optics
(BGO) or
diffraction regime
[cf. Eqs. (
16
)]
[
21
,
19
,
22
]
. Whilst BGO stands for a regime where interference is described by the nonvanishing subleading amplitudes in the JWKB approximation, the general formalism still assumes that fields satisfy systems of second-order homogeneous linear equations of motion
[
22
]
.

In this work, we propose a general framework to describe a set of tensor fields that satisfy coupled second-order linear homogeneous equations of wave-like form [
viz.
, Eqs. (
3a
) and (
3b
)]. We employ JWKB Ansätze for the fields involved and deduce several results including some ones already appearing in the known literature regarding GO and BGO in metric-affine spacetimes (which include Lorentzian spacetimes as a subcategory)
[
23
]
. We show that those known results are quite universal and directly related to special forms for the kinetic tensors in the equations of motion. Particularly, the leading order results appearing therein, which lead to the first set of GO relations, are shown to be independent of what we refer to, in the equations of motion, as friction and mass tensors. Furthermore, the first subleading order result, which is also part of GO, is completely independent of the form of the mass tensors involved. On the flipside, even for simplified kinetic tensors, higher subleading order results are still generally dependent on kinetic, friction and mass tensors. We then provide several examples in Lorentzian spacetimes, and illustrate the reach of our derivations also for two distinct families of metric-affine spacetimes.

We structure this work as follows. In Sec.
II
we present the unified framework, introducing the equations of motion and the JWKB Ansätze considered to derive the general results leading to algebraic and differential constraints between the different fields’ amplitudes and phase functions. In Sec.
III
we write a decomposition of the general kinetic tensors and explore simplified expressions for them in Subsecs.
III.1
,
III.2
and
III.3
, which allows us to further interpret the results of the prior section and establish universal results mentioned above for both GO and BGO. We then use Sec.
IV
to illustrate the latter results and the utility of our general framework applied to the dynamics of different rank fields in Lorentzian spacetimes, including the Klein-Gordon field, the electromagnetic field (both in its potential and Faraday tensor representations), gravitational waves in general relativity and reduced Horndeski theories (RHTs), the latter being an important example of modified scalar-tensor gravity having significant interest in the recent literature
[
24
,
25
,
26
,
27
]
. In Sec.
V
we apply our general results to two distinct familes of metric-affine spacetimes, namely, one possessing a metric-compatible connection and a completely antisymmetric torsion, and another referred to as a Weyl spacetime. We conclude in Sec.
VI
with a discussion of our results and future perspectives. App.
A
is dedicated to a quick review of metric-affine spacetimes.

All our derivations assume a generic metric-affine spacetime,
i.e.
, a triple
(\mathscr{M},g_{\alpha\beta},\mathchoice{\Gamma^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha\beta}}}}{\Gamma^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha\beta}}}}{\Gamma^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha\beta}}}}{\Gamma^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha\beta}}}})
, where
\mathscr{M}
is a 4-dimensional smooth manifold,
g_{\alpha\beta}
, a Lorentzian metric with signature
+2
, and
\mathchoice{\Gamma^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha\beta}}}}{\Gamma^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha\beta}}}}{\Gamma^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha\beta}}}}{\Gamma^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha\beta}}}}
is a generic affine connection (not necessarily the Levi-Civita one). We use a wide hat over a kernel letter to denote that the corresponding geometric object is computed using the Levi-Civita connection of a given spacetime. We also choose natural units to set
c=\hbar=1
, and whenever background fields are considered, a bar under a kernel letter is employed to denote that a quantity should be evaluated on a background field (or a set of background fields).

II
Multifield equations of motion and JWKB Ansätze

Let us start by considering an arbitrary set of tensor fields

\underset{I}{\Psi}\mathchoice{{}^{{{A_{I}}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.98819pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93819pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{A_{I}}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.98819pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93819pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{A_{I}}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.98819pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93819pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{A_{I}}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.98819pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93819pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}
with respect to
x^{\mu}
, though they might depend on other prescribed tensors, such as those constructed from zeroth and/or higher-order derivatives of the metric and affine connection. We refer to them, respectively, as
kinetic
,
friction
and
mass
tensors, following the usual terminology, which appears, for instance, in
[
25
]
. While we restrict ourselves to systems of linear equations of motion, this same nomenclature applies to systems of nonlinear equations of motion in the fields of interest, such as the full set of quasi-linear Einstein field equations in general relativity. For the sake of clarity, in all our derivations, whenever an equation includes a sum over several fields, we explicitly include a summation symbol with an uppercase Latin index (
J
for example). Furthermore, we use a condensed version of the usual Einstein summation convention on Greek (spacetime) indices:

\Pi(r_{I},s_{I})
,
\Pi(r_{I},s_{I})\otimes T_{\mathscr{P}}\mathscr{M}
and
\Pi(r_{I},s_{I})\otimes T_{\mathscr{P}}\mathscr{M}\otimes T_{\mathscr{P}}\mathscr{M}
, respectively. As such, for
I=J
, the proposed system of equations can only be used if the rank of the equation of motion for a given field is the same as the rank of the field itself. In particular, the formalism we derive herein cannot be used to describe the Faraday tensor through the usual first-order Maxwell equations directly (cf. Subsec.
IV.1
), which is addressed in references such as
[
28
]
. Furthermore, when
A_{I}=B_{J}
,
i.e.
, the
I
-th field has the same covariant and contravariant indices of the
J
-th field, the indices below each operator permit the distinction between otherwise ambiguous functions. For example, for a given pair
(I,J)
,
\underset{I}{\Psi}\mathchoice{{}^{{{A_{I}}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.98819pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93819pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{A_{I}}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.98819pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93819pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{A_{I}}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.98819pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93819pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{A_{I}}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.98819pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93819pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}=\underset{I}{\Psi}\mathchoice{{}^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0 and
\underset{J}{\Psi}\mathchoice{{}^{{{A_{J}}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.70232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.70232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.45242pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.40242pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{A_{J}}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.70232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.70232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.45242pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.40242pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{A_{J}}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.70232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.70232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.45242pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.40242pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{A_{J}}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.70232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.70232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.45242pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.40242pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}=\underset{J}{\Psi}\mathchoice{{}^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0 , we can still have
\underset{IJ}{\mathcal{L}}\mathchoice{{}^{{{A_{I}}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.0714pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.0714pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.73994pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.60779pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.98819pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93819pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B_{J}}}}}{{}^{{{A_{I}}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.0714pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.0714pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.73994pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.60779pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.98819pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93819pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B_{J}}}}}{{}^{{{A_{I}}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.0714pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.0714pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.73994pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.60779pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.98819pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93819pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B_{J}}}}}{{}^{{{A_{I}}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.0714pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.0714pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.73994pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.60779pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.98819pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93819pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B_{J}}}}}=\underset{IJ}{\mathcal{L}}\mathchoice{{}^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{{}^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{{}^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{{}^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}\neq\underset{JI}{\mathcal{L}}\mathchoice{{}^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{{}^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{{}^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4. le$}}{\makebox[5.60779pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.98819pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93819pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B_{J}}}}}
. In most cases (even though this is not necessary), Eqs. (
3b
) may be understood as coming from the variation of the action of the given theory with respect to the
I
-th field.

Let us then propose the following Ansätze:

\underset{I}{\Psi}\mathchoice{{}^{{{A_{I}}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.98819pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93819pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{A_{I}}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.98819pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93819pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{A_{I}}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.98819pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93819pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{A_{I}}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.98819pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93819pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}(x,\epsilon_{I})=\underset{I}{\psi}{}^{A_{I}}(x,\epsilon_{I})\,e^{iS_{I}(x)/\epsilon_{I}}\,,

(7a)

where

\underset{I}{\psi}{}^{A_{I}}(x,\epsilon_{I})\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\cdot$}}=\left[\,\sum_{p=0}^{N_{I}}\underset{I}{\psi}{}^{A_{I}}_{(p)}(x)\,\left(\frac{\epsilon_{I}}{i}\right)^{p}\,\right]\,,\quad N_{I}\geq 0\,.

(7b)

Here, each tensor field is assumed to have its own set of complex-valued tensorial
amplitude components

\left\{\underset{I}{\psi}{}^{A_{I}}_{(0)},...,\underset{I}{\psi}{}^{A_{I}}_{(N_{I})}\right\}
, grouped in the formal polynomial of Eq. (
7b
) to constitute the field
amplitude
, a real-valued scalar
phase

S_{I}(x)
, and a real-valued positive
smallness parameter

\epsilon_{I}
, a dimensionless quantity proportional to the wavelength seen by a reference frame in the open set
\mathscr{O}
where that field is defined. The gradient fields

\underset{I}{q}\mathchoice{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\cdot$}}=\partial_{\alpha}S_{I}

(8)

are assumed to be nonzero everywhere on
\mathscr{O}

[
16
]
, such that the integral curves of each
\underset{I}{q}\mathchoice{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}
form a congruence there in the open region of spacetime we are interested in,
i.e.
, through each point passes one, and only one such curve. Naturally, despite not appearing in the above equations, we have to take the real part of the right-hand side (RHS) in each JWKB Ansatz to obtain the actual fields. Also, to distinguish between the full field and its amplitude components, we use the same kernel letter but uppercase for the former, and lowercase for the latter. By analogy with what we can consider for a scalar wave in Minkowski spacetime, we assume that there exist length scales

\underset{I}{L}\mathchoice{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.2917pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.2917pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.19182pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.27986pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\psi}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.2917pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.2917pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.19182pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.27986pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\psi}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.2917pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.2917pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.19182pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.27986pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\psi}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.2917pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.2917pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.19182pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.27986pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\psi}}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\cdot$}}=}}\underset{\alpha,A_{I},B_{I}}{\text{min}}\left(\frac{|\underset{I}{\psi}{}^{A_{I}}(x)|}{|\partial_{\alpha}\underset{I}{\psi}{}^{B_{I}}(x)|}\right)

(9)

and

\underset{I}{L}\mathchoice{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.36322pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.688pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{q}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.36322pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.688pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{q}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.36322pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.688pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{q}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.36322pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.688pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{q}}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\cdot$}}=}}\underset{\alpha,\beta,\gamma}{\text{min}}\left(\frac{|\underset{I}{q}\mathchoice{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}(x)|}{|\partial_{\beta}\underset{I}{q}\mathchoice{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\gam (10)

which, respectively, represent the typical lengths of variation for the amplitude and phase of every field
I
. Also, in a general curved spacetime, metric, torsion, and nonmetricity play the roles of “refractive indices” of sorts
[
21
]
, introducing three length scales:

L_{g}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\cdot$}}=}}\underset{\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\lambda,\sigma}{\text{min}}\left(\frac{|g_{\alpha\beta}(x)|}{|\partial_{\gamma}g_{\lambda\sigma}(x)|}\right)\,,

(11)

L_{T}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\cdot$}}=}}\underset{\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\lambda,\sigma,\mu,\nu}{\text{min}}\left(\frac{|\mathchoice{T^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.73492pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.73492pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.30843pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.79175pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta\gamma}}}}{T^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.73492pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.73492pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.30843pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.79175pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta\gamma}}}}{T^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.73492pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.73492pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.30843pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.79175pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta\gamma}}}}{T^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.73492pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.73492pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.30843pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.79175pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta\gamma}}}}(x)|}{|\partial_{\lambda}\mathchoice{T^{{{\sigma}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.6 (12)

L_{Q}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\cdot$}}=}}\underset{\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\lambda,\sigma,\mu,\nu}{\text{min}}\left(\frac{|\mathchoice{Q^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.93369pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.93369pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.44296pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.0307pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\alpha\beta\gamma}}}}{Q^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.93369pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.93369pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.44296pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.0307pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\alpha\beta\gamma}}}}{Q^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.93369pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.93369pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.44296pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.0307pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\alpha\beta\gamma}}}}{Q^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.93369pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.93369pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.44296pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.0307pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\alpha\beta\gamma}}}}(x)|}{|\partial_{\lambda}\mathchoice{Q^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.50554pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.50554pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.17293pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83778pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\sigma\mu\nu}}}}{Q^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.50554pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.50554pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.17293pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83778pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\sigma\mu\nu}}}}{Q^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.50554pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.50554pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.17293pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83778pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\sigma\mu\nu}}}}{Q^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.50554pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.50554pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.17293pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83778pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\sigma\mu\nu}}}}(x)|}\right)\,,

(13)

which, together with
\underset{I}{L}\mathchoice{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.2917pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.2917pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.19182pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.27986pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\psi}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.2917pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.2917pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.19182pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.27986pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\psi}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.2917pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.2917pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.19182pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.27986pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\psi}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.2917pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.2917pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.19182pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.27986pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\psi}}}}
and
\underset{I}{L}\mathchoice{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.36322pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.688pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{q}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.36322pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.688pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{q}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.36322pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.688pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{q}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.36322pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.688pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{q}}}}
, are to be taken into account when characterizing the desired regime of validity for the proposed solution (
14a
). We then define the smallness parameter of field
I
as
\epsilon_{I}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\cdot$}}=\lambda_{I}/L_{I}
, where
\lambda_{I}
is its wavelength measured by a reference frame
u^{\mu}(x)

[
29
]
, and
L_{I}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\cdot$}}=\text{min}\{\underset{I}{L}\mathchoice{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.2917pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.2917pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.19182pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.27986pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\psi}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.2917pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.2917pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.19182pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.27986pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\psi}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.2917pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.2917pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.19182pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.27986pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\psi}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.2917pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.2917pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.19182pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.27986pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\psi}}}},\underset{I}{L}\mathchoice{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.36322pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.688pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{q}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.36322pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.688pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{q}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.36322pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.688pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{q}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.36322pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.688pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{q}}}},L_{g},L_{T},L_{Q}\}
, such that there are scenarios where
\epsilon_{I}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\ll}}1
,
i.e.
,
\lambda_{I}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\ll}}L_{I}
. It is worth mentioning that the characteristic length scales
\{\underset{I}{L}\mathchoice{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.2917pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.2917pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.19182pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.27986pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\psi}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.2917pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.2917pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.19182pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.27986pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\psi}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.2917pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.2917pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.19182pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.27986pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\psi}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.2917pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.2917pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.19182pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.27986pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\psi}}}},\underset{I}{L}\mathchoice{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.36322pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.688pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{q}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.36322pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.688pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{q}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.36322pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.688pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{q}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.91634pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.36322pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.688pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{q}}}},L_{g},L_{T},L_{Q}\}
are loosely defined by Eqs. (
9
) through (
13
), respectively, but it is also common to see some of them appearing in the form of tensor quantities depending on the application under investigation. Indeed, for Lorentzian spacetimes,
L_{g}
may be identified as one of the following curvature related scalars:
\widehat{R}^{-1/2}
,
(\widehat{R}_{\mu\nu}\widehat{R}^{\mu\nu})^{-1/4}
or
(\widehat{R}_{\mu\nu\sigma\rho}\widehat{R}^{\mu\nu\sigma\rho})^{-1/4}
, where
\widehat{R}
,
\widehat{R}_{\mu\nu}
and
\widehat{R}_{\mu\nu\sigma\rho}
are, respectively, the Ricci scalar, the Ricci tensor, and the Riemann tensor. For spacetimes with
\widehat{R}_{\mu\nu}\neq 0
, the first two choices are reasonable candidates, whereas in the case, for example, of gravitational waves considered up to linear order on a Ricci-flat background, the latter is a better measurement of curvature, since the Kretschmann scalar
\widehat{R}_{\mu\nu\sigma\rho}\widehat{R}^{\mu\nu\sigma\rho}
is the only nonvanishing quantity in that case
[
20
,
30
]
.

While including different smallness parameters for distinct fields is surely a more general approach to follow, for simplicity, we choose to reexpress all Ansätze in terms of a single control parameter. We thus use Eqs. (
7a
) and (
7b
) as an inspiration to consider alternative Ansätze expressed in terms of a unique smallness parameter,
\epsilon
:

\underset{I}{\Psi}\mathchoice{{}^{{{A_{I}}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.98819pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93819pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{A_{I}}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.98819pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93819pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{A_{I}}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.98819pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93819pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{A_{I}}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.98819pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93819pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}(x,\epsilon)=\underset{I}{\psi}{}^{A_{I}}(x,\epsilon)\,e^{iS_{I}(x)/\epsilon}\,,

(14a)

with

\underset{I}{\psi}{}^{A_{I}}(x,\epsilon)\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\cdot$}}=\left[\,\sum_{p=0}^{N}\underset{I}{\psi}{}^{A_{I}}_{(p)}(x)\,\left(\frac{\epsilon}{i}\right)^{p}\,\right]\,,\quad N\geq 0\,,

(14b)

where

N\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\cdot$}}=\underset{I}{\text{max}}\,\{N_{I}\}\,,

(14c)

and
\underset{I}{\psi}{}^{A_{I}}_{(p)}=0
for
p>N_{I}
if
N_{I}<N
. In these alternative Ansätze, we have ordered the fields
\underset{I}{\Psi}\mathchoice{{}^{{{A_{I}}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.98819pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93819pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{A_{I}}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.98819pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93819pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{A_{I}}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.98819pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93819pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{A_{I}}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.98819pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93819pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}
such that the
M
-tuple
(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},...,\epsilon_{M})
satisfies
\epsilon_{1}\leq\epsilon_{2}\leq\cdots\leq\epsilon_{M}
, and
\epsilon
is supposed to lie somewhere in the interval
[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{M}]
. As such, if
\epsilon
is small, all fields with
\epsilon_{I}\leq\epsilon
will require only equations involving their leading-order amplitudes
\underset{I}{\psi}{}^{A_{I}}_{(0)}
to have most of their dynamics described, a situation which we refer to as the
geometrical optics regime
(GO), whereas the description of other fields may demand that higher-order amplitude components
\underset{I}{\psi}{}^{A_{I}}_{(p)}
(
p=1,...,P_{I}>0
) should be taken into account, a broader situation which we refer to as the
diffraction regime
or
beyond geometrical optics
(BGO). With these transformations, the
wave vector
associated with field
I
is given by

\underset{I}{k}\mathchoice{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\cdot$}}=\epsilon^{-1}\,\underset{I}{q}\mathchoice{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}=\epsilon^{-1}\,\partial_{\mu}S_{I}\,.

(15)

14a
) into Eq. (
3a
) and demanding its validity for each order of
\epsilon

(16a)

,
\underset{IJ}{\mathcal{T}}\mathchoice{{}^{{{A_{I}}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.0714pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.0714pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.73994pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.60779pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.98819pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93819pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B_{J}}}}}{{}^{{{A_{I}}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.0714pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.0714pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.73994pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.60779pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.98819pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93819pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B_{J}}}}}{{}^{{{A_{I}}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.0714pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.0714pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.73994pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.60779pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.98819pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93819pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B_{J}}}}}{{}^{{{A_{I}}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.0714pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.0714pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.73994pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.60779pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.98819pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93819pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B_{J}}}}}
and
\underset{IJ}{\mathcal{L}}\mathchoice{{}^{{{A_{I}}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.0714pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.0714pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.73994pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.60779pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.98819pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93819pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B_{J}}}}}{{}^{{{A_{I}}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.0714pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.0714pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.73994pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.60779pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.98819pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93819pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B_{J}}}}}{{}^{{{A_{I}}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.0714pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.0714pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.73994pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.60779pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.98819pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93819pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B_{J}}}}}{{}^{{{A_{I}}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.0714pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.0714pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.73994pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.60779pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.98819pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93819pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B_{J}}}}}
on
\underset{I}{q}\mathchoice{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}
and on covariant derivatives, Eqs. (
20b
) and (
20c
) can be understood as a system of
perturbative
evolution equations for the different order amplitudes of all fields involved, in which the behavior of
\underset{I}{\psi}{}^{A_{I}}_{(p+1)}
is influenced only by
\underset{J}{\psi}{}^{A_{I}}_{(p)}
, not by
\underset{J}{\psi}{}^{A_{I}}_{(p+2)}
. Furthermore, the results concerning the leading-order amplitudes
\underset{J}{\psi}{}^{A_{J}}_{(0)}
are indifferent to amplitudes beyond geometrical optics being present or not. Finally, in the special case in which all fields share a single phase,
i.e.
,
S_{I}(x)=S(x)\,\,(1\leq I\leq M)
, but not necessarily
\underset{IJ}{\mathcal{D}}\mathchoice{{}^{{{A_{I}}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.0714pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.0714pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.73994pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.60779pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.98819pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93819pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B_{J}}}}}{{}^{{{A_{I}}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.0714pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.0714pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.73994pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.60779pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.98819pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93819pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B_{J}}}}}{{}^{{{A_{I}}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.0714pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.0714pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.73994pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.60779pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.98819pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93819pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B_{J}}}}}{{}^{{{A_{I}}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.0714pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.0714pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.73994pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.60779pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.05238pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.98819pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93819pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B_{J}}}}}=0
holds, the exponentials appearing in Eqs. (
16a
), (
16b
) and (
16c
) may be naturally factored out.

III
Decomposition of the kinetic tensor

While the above results are quite general and independent of the tensor field(s) considered, it is interesting to start extracting information from Eqs. (
16a
) through (
16c
) by performing the following decomposition of the kinetic tensor

satisfying the constraints

(21b)

This splits the kinetic tensor into a part that does not mix different fields (
field-diagonal
or F-diagonal), represented by the term within round brackets, and a remainder which gives the coupling between different fields (the kinetic terms in the
I
-th equation of motion that depend on the
J
-th field, with
J\neq I
), and allows us to identify in what follows the conditions leading to the well-known results of GO and BGO in literature
[
31
,
20
]
. Furthermore, the F-diagonal term is additionally split in two, one proportional to
g^{\alpha\beta}
(
metric-diagonal
or G-diagonal), which generally leads to the d’Alembertian operator appearing in wave-like equations of motion for various theories (cf. Sec.
IV
), and a remainder, which is only F-diagonal. We now use this decomposition to derive the results of having simpler types of kinetic tensors isolatedly.

III.1
FG-diagonal kinetic tensor

First, we consider an
FG-diagonal
kinetic tensor,
i.e.
,

, but

. Then, Eq. (

to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\cdot$}}\underset{I}{\overset{(2)}{\varkappa}}\mathchoice{{}^{{{\alpha\beta}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{\alpha\beta}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{\alpha\beta}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{\alpha\beta}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{}\,\delta^{A_{I}}_{B_{I}}\,,

(27)

where

\delta^{A_{I}}_{B_{I}}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\cdot$}}=\delta^{\mu_{1}}_{\sigma_{1}}\cdots\delta^{\mu_{r_{I}}}_{\sigma_{r_{I}}}\delta_{\nu_{1}}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots\delta_{\nu_{s_{I}}}^{\lambda_{s_{I}}}

(28)

is the identity operator in the tensor space
\Pi_{\mathscr{P}}(r_{I},s_{I})
for every
\mathscr{P}\in\mathscr{M}
. We refer to this possibility as
FC-diagonality
, since the F-diagonal part of the kinetic tensor does not mix different components of a given field. Then, Eq. (
16a
) gives

\left(\underset{I}{\overset{(1)}{\varkappa}}\mathchoice{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}\,g^{\alpha\beta}+\underset{I}{\overset{(2)}{\varkappa}}\mathchoice{{}^{{{\alpha\beta}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{\alpha\beta}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scr a nontrivial example of vanishing dispersion tensors [cf. Eq. (

(32b)

for
0\leq p\leq N
.

III.3
FCG-diagonal kinetic tensor

If
\underset{I}{\overset{(2)}{\varkappa}}\mathchoice{{}^{{{\alpha\beta}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{\alpha\beta}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{\alpha\beta}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{\alpha\beta}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}=0
, the kinetic tensor is FCG-diagonal, implying that
\mathcal{C}_{I}=0
and that
\underset{I}{q}\mathchoice{{}^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}
satisfies Eq. (
24
). In turn, Eqs. (
20b
) and (
20c
) simplify to the following:

\underset{I}{\mathscr{D}}\mathchoice{{}^{{{(g)}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.40248pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.40248pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.32404pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.51718pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{(g)}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.40248pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.40248pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.32404pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.51718pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{(g)}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.40248pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.40248pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.32404pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.51718pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{(g)}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.40248pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.40248pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.32404pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.51718pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}\underset{I}{\psi}{}^{A_{I}}_{(0)}+\sum_{J=1}^{M}\,\frac{\underset{IJ}{\mathcal{F}}\mathchoice{{}^{{{\alpha (33a)

and
