0
), which we refer to as MCCAT, by analogy with the Lorentzian case, we can use the primitive first-order Maxwell equations as a starting point and recast them in terms of the full affine connection
\mathchoice{\Gamma^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}}{\Gamma^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}}{\Gamma^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}}{\Gamma^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}}
:

\widehat{\nabla}_{\nu}F^{\mu\nu}=\nabla_{\nu}F^{\mu\nu}-\frac{1}{2}\mathchoice{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha\beta}}}}{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha\beta}}}}{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha\beta}}}}{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha\beta}}}}\,F^{\alpha\beta}=0\,,

to derive the following wave-like equation of motion for

(56)

where we recall that the original Maxwell equations need to be further enforced on the solutions of the previous equations to remove nonphysical solutions. A quick inspection shows that the kinetic, friction and mass tensors are, respectively, given by:

(58)

The Ansatz is equal to the one for the Lorentzian case, namely, Eq. (
38
) applied to
F_{\mu\nu}
. Now, even with a more complicated equation of motion, the kinetic tensor here is also FCG-diagonal, such that the wave vector here as well is
null
[cf. Eq. (
23
)] and
affinely parametrized affine and metric geodesic
[cf. the comment after Eq. (
24
)]
[
23
]
. This condition on the kinetic tensor and the nonzero friction tensor for MCCAT lead to the following transport equation for the leading-order amplitude
f_{(0)\mu\nu}
[cf. Eq. (

(60)

Now, the gauge conditions to be imposed on the solutions above are given by Eqs. (
54
) and (
55
), implying that
\underset{}{f}_{(0)\mu\nu}
is transverse to the wave vector
k^{\mu}
. Therefore, the
laws of geometrical optics
for the Faraday tensor herein read:

(i)

the wave vector
k^{\mu}
is null and (affine and metric) geodesic,

(ii)

the leading-order amplitude
\underset{}{f}_{(0)\mu\nu}
is transverse to
k^{\mu}
, and

(iii)

\underset{}{f}_{(0)\mu\nu}
is transported according to Eq. (
59
), which in the case of MCCAT has an additional friction tensor.

Finally, subleading-order amplitudes
\underset{}{f}_{(p)\mu\nu}
(
1\leq p\leq N
) satisfy Eq. (
33b
) for the MCCAT case:

&+\nabla_{[\mu}\mathchoice{T^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.27318pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.27318pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.78163pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.70114pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda\sigma}}}_{{{\nu]}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.37701pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.37701pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.65826pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.04161pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{T^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.27318pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.27318pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.78163pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.70114pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda\sigma}}}_{{{\nu]}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.37701pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.37701pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.65826pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.04161pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{T^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.27318pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.27318pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.78163pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.70114pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda\sigma}}}_{{{\nu]}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.37701pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.37701pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.65826pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.04161pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{T^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.27318pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.27318pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.78163pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.70114pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda\sigma}}}_{{{\nu]}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.37701pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.37701pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.65826pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.04161pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}+\nabla^{\sigma}\mathchoice{T^{{{\lambda}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}}{T^{{{\lambda}\mathchoice (62)

Eq. (
59
) shows that
\underset{}{f}_{(0)\mu\nu}
evolves independently of the higher-order amplitudes
\underset{}{f}_{(p)\mu\nu}
(
1\leq p\leq N
), similar to all the Lorentzian examples we considered in Sec.
IV
. Additionally, Eqs. (
61
) are
beyond geometrical optics
transport equations for
\underset{}{f}_{(p)\mu\nu}
(
1\leq p\leq N
) along the integral curves of
k^{\mu}
. These also comprise a set of perturbative equations, allowing one to obtain higher-order corrections to the amplitude of the Ansatz of
F_{\mu\nu}
, thus refining the full JWKB amplitude beyond its leading-order component
\underset{}{f}_{(0)\mu\nu}
.

V.2
Faraday tensor in Weyl spacetimes

In a spacetime with a symmetric connection (
\mathchoice{T^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}}{T^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}}{T^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}}{T^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}}=0
) and a Weyl nonmetricity (
\mathchoice{Q^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.07036pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.07036pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.50754pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.0768pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu\alpha}}}}{Q^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.07036pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.07036pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.50754pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.0768pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu\alpha}}}}{Q^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.07036pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.07036pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.50754pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.0768pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu\alpha}}}}{Q^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.07036pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.07036pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.50754pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.0768pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu\alpha}}}}=g_{\mu\nu}\,\zeta_{\alpha}
, where
\zeta_{\alpha}
is an arbitrary real 1-form), we continue to use Maxwell equations as a starting point and recast them in terms of the full affine connection
\mathchoice{\Gamma^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}}{\Gamma^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}}{\Gamma^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}}{\Gamma^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}}

\widehat{\nabla}_{\nu}F^{\mu\nu}=\nabla_{\nu}F^{\mu\nu}+2\zeta_{\nu}\,F^{\mu\nu}=0\,,

(63)

\partial_{[\lambda}F_{\mu\nu]}=\nabla_{[\lambda}F_{\mu\nu]}=0\,.

(64)

These imply that the Faraday tensor satisfies the following wave-like equation of motion:

\square\,F_{\mu\nu}+2\zeta_{[\mu}\,g^{\alpha\sigma}\delta^{\lambda}_{\nu]}\,\nabla_{\alpha}F_{\lambda\sigma}+2\left(\mathchoice{R^{{{\lambda}\mathchoice{\makebox[13.3994pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.3994pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.09523pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.78227pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\sigma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{[\mu\nu]}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{R^{{{\lambda}\mathchoice{\makebox[13.3994pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.3994pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.09523pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.78227pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\sigma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{[\mu\nu]}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{R^{{{\lambda}\mathchoice{\makebox[13.3994pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.3994pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.09523pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.78227pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\sigma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{[\mu\nu]}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{R^{{{\lambda}\mathchoice{\makebox[13.3994pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.3994pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.09523pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.78227pt][c]{$ (65)

A quick inspection shows that the kinetic, friction and mass tensors in this case are, respectively, given by:

\underset{}{\mathcal{K}}\mathchoice{{}^{{{\alpha\beta}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}}{{}^{{{\alpha\beta}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}}{{}^{{{\alpha\beta}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}}{{}^{{{\alpha\beta}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}}\mathchoice{{}^{{{\lambda\sigma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.37701pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.37701pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.65826pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.04161pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{{}^{{{\lambda\sigma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.37701pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.37701pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.65826pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.04161pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{{}^{{{\lambda\s (66)

\underset{}{\mathcal{M}}\mathchoice{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}}\mathchoice{{}^{{{\lambda\sigma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.37701pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.37701pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.65826pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.04161pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{{}^{{{\lambda\sigma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.37701pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.37701pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.65826pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.04161pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{{}^{{{\lambda\sigma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.37701pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.37701pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\ma (67)

The proposed Ansatz is equal to the one for the Lorentzian case and the first metric-affine geometry we have considered. Here as well, the kinetic tensor is FCG-diagonal, such that the wave vector is also
null
[cf. Eq. (
23
)]. However, contrary to MCCAT, for a Weyl connection, Eq. (
24
) implies that integral curves of
\underset{}{k}\mathchoice{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}
are affinely parametrized metric geodesics but nonaffinely parametrized autoparallels [cf. again the comment after Eq. (
24
)]. Finally, the leading-order of Eq. (
33a
), given that we also have a nonzero friction, yields

\underset{}{\mathscr{D}}\mathchoice{{}^{{{(g)}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.40248pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.40248pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.32404pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.51718pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{(g)}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.40248pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.40248pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.32404pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.51718pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{(g)}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.40248pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.40248pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.32404pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.51718pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{(g)}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.40248pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.40248pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.32404pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.51718pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}\underset{}{f}_{(0)\mu\nu}+2\zeta_{[\mu}\,k^{\sigma}\delta^{\lambda}_{\nu]}\,\underset{}{f}_{(0)\lambda\sigma}=0\,,

(68)

whereas Eqs.(
33b
) leads to the transport equations of subleading-order amplitudes:

\underset{}{\mathscr{D}}\mathchoice{{}^{{{(g)}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.40248pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.40248pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.32404pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.51718pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{(g)}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.40248pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.40248pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.32404pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.51718pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{(g)}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.40248pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.40248pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.32404pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.51718pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{(g)}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.40248pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.40248pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.32404pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.51718pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}\underset{}{f}_{(p+1)\mu\nu}+2\zeta_{[\mu}\,k^{\sigma}\delta^{\lambda}_{\nu]}\,\underset{}{f}_{(p+1)\lambda\sigma}=-\underset{}{\mathcal{L}}\mathchoice{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}}\mathchoice{{}^{{{\lambda\sigma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.37701pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.37701pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.65826pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.04161pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{{}^{{{\lambda\sigma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.37701pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.37701pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.65826pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.04161pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{{}^{{{\lambda\sigma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.37701pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.37701pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.65826pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.04161pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{{}^{{{\lambda\sigma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.37701pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.37701pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.65826pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.04161pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\,\underset{}{f}_{(p)\lambda\sigma}\,,\quad(0\geq p\geq N)

(69)

with

\underset{}{\mathscr{D}}\mathchoice{{}^{{{(g)}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.40248pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.40248pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.32404pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.51718pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{(g)}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.40248pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.40248pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.32404pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.51718pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{(g)}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.40248pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.40248pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.32404pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.51718pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{(g)}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.40248pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.40248pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.32404pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.51718pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}=2\,\underset{}{k}\mathchoice{{}^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebo and

\underset{}{\mathcal{L}}\mathchoice{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}}\mathchoice{{}^{{{\lambda\sigma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.37701pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.37701pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.65826pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.04161pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{{}^{{{\lambda\sigma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.37701pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.37701pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.65826pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.04161pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{{}^{{{\lambda\sigma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.37701pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.37701pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\ma (71)

Finally, by imposing Maxwell equations as gauge conditions on the above solutions shows that
\underset{}{f}_{(0)\mu\nu}
is transverse to the wave vector. Similar to the MCCAT case, the above constraints imply that Eq. (
68
) simplifies to

\underset{}{\mathscr{D}}\mathchoice{{}^{{{(g)}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.40248pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.40248pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.32404pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.51718pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{(g)}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.40248pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.40248pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.32404pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.51718pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{(g)}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.40248pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.40248pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.32404pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.51718pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{(g)}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.40248pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.40248pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.32404pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.51718pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}\underset{}{f}_{(0)\mu\nu}=0\,,

(72)

where the above differential operator resembles the one appearing in the Lorentzian examples, but, in Weyl spacetimes, the 1-form
\zeta_{\alpha}
modifies the simpler
\underset{}{\widehat{\mathscr{D}}}\mathchoice{{}^{{{(g)}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.40248pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.40248pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.32404pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.51718pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{(g)}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.40248pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.40248pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.32404pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.51718pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{(g)}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.40248pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.40248pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.32404pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.51718pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}{{}^{{{(g)}\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.40248pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.40248pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.32404pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.51718pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{}}}}
.

Accordingly, the
laws of geometrical optics
for the Faraday tensor read:

(i)

the wave vector
k^{\mu}
is null and (affine and metric) geodesic,

(ii)

the leading-order amplitude
\underset{}{f}_{(0)\mu\nu}
is transverse to
k^{\mu}
, and

(iii)

\underset{}{f}_{(0)\mu\nu}
is transported according to Eq. (
68
).

Eq.(
68
) shows that
\underset{}{f}_{(0)\mu\nu}
evolves independently of the higher-order amplitudes
\underset{}{f}_{(p)\mu\nu}
(
1\leq p\leq N
). In turn, Eqs. (
69
) are
beyond geometrical optics
transport equations for
\underset{}{f}_{(p)\mu\nu}
(
1\leq p\leq N
) along the integral curves of
k^{\mu}
. These also comprise a set of perturbative equations, allowing one to obtain higher-order corrections to the amplitude of the Ansatz of
F_{\mu\nu}
, thus refining the full JWKB amplitude beyond its leading-order component
\underset{}{f}_{(0)\mu\nu}
.

Figure 2:
Venn diagram representing the different possible systems of second-order (nonvanishing kinetic tensor) homogeneous equations of motion and the presence or absence of friction and mass tensors. All examples in Secs.
IV
and
V
appear in relevant intersections among the sets considered.

VI
Discussion

In this work, we have developed a unified framework for describing linear waves associated to tensor fields of arbitrary rank obeying coupled homogeneous linear wave-like second-order PDEs. Even though Eqs. (
16a
) through (
16c
) are quite straightforwardly derived from inserting the Ansätze (
14a
) into Eqs. (
3a
) and demanding that the latter are valid for each and every order of
\epsilon
, those relations are the core results leading to the well-known relations of GO and BGO appearing in the literature.

More specifically, GO is derived from the leading (
p=0
) and first subleading-orders (
p=1
) of those equations, which give, respectively, definite constraints among the vector fields
\underset{I}{q}\mathchoice{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}
and transport equations for every leading-order amplitude
\underset{I}{\psi}{}^{A_{I}}_{(0)}
, whereas BGO relates to the transport equations for the (
p\geq 1
)-order amplitudes. Furthermore, the GO results only depend on the kinetic and friction tensors appearing in Eqs.(
3a
). This is precisely why we considered the decomposition of a generic kinetic tensor in Sec.
III
, which allowed us to establish important results directly dependent on its form, namely, F-diagonality, FG-diagonality, FC-diagonality, and FCG-diagonality.

As we have seen, provided that
\underset{I}{\psi}{}^{A_{I}}_{(0)}
vanishes at most in isolated points, either FG-diagonality or FCG-diagonality for the kinetic tensor lead to
\underset{I}{q}\mathchoice{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}
being null, which, together with
\underset{I}{q}\mathchoice{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}{{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}
being a gradient, yields Eq. (
24
) as its corresponding transport law. This is one of the core results derived from the unified framework we present herein, showing that specific forms for the kinetic tensor—which in the language of characteristic surfaces and bi-characteristic curves
[
9
]
correspond to the so-called
principal part
of the associated equations of motion—directly lead to a generalization of one of the key results of traditional GO, namely, that rays are
null geodesics

[
13
,
32
,
18
,
16
,
14
]
.

This result applies to all examples in Lorentzian spacetimes we have considered in Sec.
IV
, with the exception of RHTs, in which the perturbation of the scalar field does not necessarily possess either an FG- or an FCG-diagonal kinetic tensor. Furthemore, given a more general affine connection, Eq. (
24
) shows that the null curves of a given physical field continue to be extremal geodesics, but no longer need to be autoparallels
[
23
]
. And even though this is the case, in Sec.
V
we gave two explicit nontrivial examples, MCCAT and Weyl spacetimes, where the null curves continue to be autoparallels (nonaffinely parametrized in the latter case).

Hence, contrary to Lorentzian geometry, where traditional GO gives the pleasant result that null curves are geodesics—which demonstrates in an alternative manner Einstein’s original hypothesis in his formulation of GR
[
35
]
—, a similar formalism applied to metric-affine gravity gives a perhaps not-so-pleasant but still consistent outcome. Nonetheless, even in the more general context of metric-affine spacetimes, provided we still assume fields satisfy linear equations of motion, the first law of geometrical optics remains independent of any amplitude
\underset{I}{\psi}{}^{A_{I}}_{(p)}
, meaning the geometry of null rays can be studied irrespective of how the amplitude of the corresponding field evolves through spacetime. This is precisely what allows us to properly study the geometry of null rays and generalize, for example, the usual distance reciprocity relation in the context of an arbitrary metric-affine spacetime
[
23
]
.

While beyond the scope of this presentation, provided the associated kinetic tensor has an FCG-diagonal form, the GO transport equation for an otherwise arbitrary
\underset{I}{\psi}{}^{A_{I}}_{(p)}
[cf. Eq. (
33a
)] can be used to derive balance equations for a phenomenological brightness-related quantity, from which a notion of particle number arises by making use of the de Broglie hypothesis. This would allow us to obtain a first principles approach to study the relativistic thermodynamic regime of such systems
[
36
]
or to deal with kinetic treatments for gravitationally induced particle production
[
37
]
. In a future work, we shall explore such phenomenological equations to derive a generalized version of the usual distance duality relation in a generic metric-affine spacetime. In particular, such generalization could be contrasted to observational data in order to constrain additional degrees of freedom in the gravitational field and/or exotic types of matter and radiation inhabiting the Universe.

VII
